Early life stress and HPA axis function independently predict

adult depressive symptoms in metropolitan Cebu, Philippines

page=1 block=1118307744 (x=158 y=144) width=108.000000 height=10.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Objectives: Alterations in adult hypothalamic–pituitary–adrenal (HPA) axis activity
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=224) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
have increasingly been linked with early life stress and adult depression, but a limited
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=239) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
number of studies have used longitudinal data to explore HPA axis dysregulation as
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=254) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
an underlying mechanism driving the long-term depressive impacts of early stressors.
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=269) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Here we address potential long-term impacts of early life, family-based stress on
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=284) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
depressive symptoms among young adults in a longitudinal birth cohort study begun
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=299) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in 1983 in the Philippines.
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=314) width=101.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Materials and methods: We relate a composite measure of family-based stressors experi-
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=329) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
enced between birth and adolescence to circadian dynamics in adult salivary cortisol and
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=344) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
depressive risk measured at 21–22 years of age. Multiple regression analyses were con-
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=359) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ducted to examine the relationship between early life stress levels and risk of adult depres-
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=374) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sive symptoms, as well as the role of adult diurnal cortisol activity in this relationship.
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=389) width=307.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Results: Greater levels of early life familial stress predicted more severe depressive
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=404) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
symptomatology at age 21–22 in a dose–response fashion (p < .0001) independent
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=419) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of adult diurnal cortisol patterns. Flatter diurnal cortisol slopes are directly associated
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=434) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with higher adult depressive symptoms, an effect mostly driven by evening cortisol
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=449) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
levels (p = .004). When considering the cumulative effects of early life stress mea-
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=464) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sures, however, exposure to more of these stressors during development is associ-
page=1 block=-672210048 (x=166 y=479) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ated with even higher depressive symptoms.
page=1 block=1892944294 (x=166 y=494) width=174.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Discussion: The long-term depressive effects of early life familial stress extend to this
page=1 block=-672235203 (x=166 y=509) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
large sample of Cebuano young adults, and early life stress and HPA axis function
page=1 block=-672235203 (x=166 y=524) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
may shape adult depressive symptoms through independent pathways in this sample.
page=1 block=-672235203 (x=166 y=539) width=333.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Our findings provide further evidence that HPA axis activity is shaped by early life
page=1 block=-672235203 (x=166 y=554) width=332.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
conditions and is associated with depressive symptoms.
page=1 block=-672235203 (x=166 y=569) width=217.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
depression, developmental origins of health and disease, early life stress, family stress, global
page=1 block=-824563026 (x=166 y=607) width=319.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
mental health, HPA axis, low- and middle-income countries, Philippines

1 | INTRODUCTION
page=1 block=7868268 (x=1 y=658) width=120.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=14
Depression accounts for a large and growing burden of disease world-
page=1 block=15936910 (x=1 y=686) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
wide and disproportionately impacts individuals living in low- and
page=1 block=15936910 (x=1 y=699) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
448
page=1 block=788592 (x=1 y=720) width=12.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=13
© 2020 Wiley Periodicals LLC

| Sonny A. Bechayda3 |
page=1 block=-1780742752 (x=278 y=144) width=138.000000 height=10.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
middle-income countries (Patel 2007; Vigo et al. 2016). Increasing evi-
page=1 block=-106598692 (x=263 y=660) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
dence suggests that physical, nutritional, and psychosocial insults
page=1 block=-106598692 (x=263 y=673) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
increase the risk of adult depression and other forms of mental and
page=1 block=-106598692 (x=263 y=686) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
physical illnesses, particularly when individuals endure these impacts
page=1 block=-106598692 (x=263 y=699) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Am JPhys Anthropol. 2020;173:448–462.

449
page=2 block=387710980 (x=493 y=1) width=12.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=13
Shattuck, Tyrka, Geracioti, & Price, 2011; Elzinga et al., 2008; Janusek,
page=2 block=15928076 (x=1 y=707) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
greatest (Patel 2007; Vigo et al. 2016). Testing this pathway in diverse
page=2 block=-122549492 (x=262 y=707) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
during early development (Barker et al. 1999; Chapman et al., 2004).
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=31) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
For example, numerous studies have consistently reported that histo-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=44) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ries of childhood stress and trauma elevate individual risk of develop-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=57) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ing depression during adulthood (Carr, Martins, Stingel, Lemgruber, &
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=70) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Juruena, 2013; Heim & Binder, 2012; Hovens et al., 2010; Kim,
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=83) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Nyengerai, & Mendenhall, 2020; Mandelli, Petrelli, & Serretti, 2015;
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=96) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Wiersma et al., 2009). The durable effects of early life psychosocial
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=109) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stress exposure are also known to predict adult depression beyond
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=122) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the influence of genetic predispositions, as these patterns have been
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=135) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
reported in twin studies (Kendler et al., 2000; Nelson et al., 2002). Elu-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=148) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
cidating the possible underlying biological correlates, in diverse cul-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=161) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tural contexts, can allow for the identification of universal biocultural
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=174) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
pathways that contribute to the lasting impacts of early life experi-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=187) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ences on adult depression and can illuminate potential opportunities
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to ameliorate the long-term poor health outcomes shaped by these
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adverse experiences and conditions.
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=226) width=125.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The hypothalamic–pituitary–adrenal (HPA) axis, a primary physio-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=16 y=238) width=228.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
logical system involved in the mammalian stress response, has been
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=252) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
implicated as an important biological system that sustains the impacts
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=265) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of early life psychosocial and environmental exposures and shapes
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=278) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
later life mental health status (Heim & Binder, 2012; Miller, Chen, &
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=291) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Parker, 2011; Taylor, Way, & Seeman, 2011). Normal HPA axis activ-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=304) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ity follows a diurnal rhythm where circulating cortisol concentrations
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
are elevated upon waking, surge post-waking to reach peak levels
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
~30 min after waking (a phenomenon known as the cortisol awaken-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=343) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ing response), and decrease across the day until reaching the body's
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=355) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lowest levels before slumber (Kirschbaum & Hellhammer, 1989;
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Pruessner et al., 1997; Weitzman et al., 1971). Early life stress expo-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sure and adverse social, emotional, and physical conditions have con-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sistently been reported as strong predictors of HPA axis dysregulation
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
as indexed by abnormal diurnal cortisol rhythms and reactions to
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
stressful events (Gustafsson, Janlert, Theorell, & Hammarström, 2010;
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Heim & Binder, 2012; Taylor et al., 2011; Thayer, Wilson, Kim, &
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Jaeggi, 2018), though the direction of these relationships has varied
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
across the literature. For example, individuals who experienced early
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
life stress have exhibited both higher (Luecken & Appelhans, 2006;
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=485) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Schalinski, Elbert, Steudte-Schmiedgen, & Kirschbaum, 2015; van der
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Vegt, Van Der Ende, Kirschbaum, Verhulst, & Tiemeier, 2009) and

lower levels of basal cortisol (DeSantis, Kuzawa, & Adam, 2015; Tri-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=524) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ckett, Noll, Susman, Shenk, & Putnam, 2010; van der Vegt
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
et al., 2009) during adulthood. Early life stress is also related to an
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
altered cortisol awakening response (Butler, Klaus, Edwards, &
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=563) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
Pennington, 2017; Engert, Efanov, Dedovic, Dagher, &
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=13
Pruessner, 2011; Fogelman & Canli, 2018; Gonzalez, Jenkins,
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
Steiner, & Fleming, 2009) and evening cortisol levels (Engert
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
et al., 2011; Gustafsson et al., 2010) in adulthood in both positive and
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
negative directions. Additionally, adults with a history of child abuse
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and neglect have been reported to exhibit signs of HPA axis dys-
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=642) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
regulation as evidenced by greater (Heim et al., 2000; Luecken &
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=654) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Appelhans, 2006; Pesonen et al., 2010; Vaccarino, Levitan, &
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
Ravindran, 2015) and blunted levels (Carnut?{t\hskip-0.7ex\char "B8}a,
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=680) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Cris?an, Vulturar, Opre, & Miu, 2015; Carpenter et al., 2007; Carpenter,
page=2 block=15945925 (x=1 y=693) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Tell, Gaylord-Harden, & Mathews, 2017; Lovallo, Farag, Sorocco,
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=31) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Cohoon, & Vincent, 2012) of cortisol reactivity in response to acute
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=44) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
psychosocial stressors. Early life stress-induced HPA-axis dys-
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=56) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
regulation has also been seen in non-human primates. Unpredictable
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=70) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
separations from the mother, unpredictable maternal feedings, or
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=83) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
spontaneous maternal abusive behavior among captive rhesus
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=95) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
macaque infants were found to predict altered diurnal cortisol
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=109) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
rhythms characterized by lower morning levels, higher daytime levels,
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=122) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and flatter cortisol slopes across the day (Coplan et al., 1996; Sánchez
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=134) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
et al., 2005). This pattern of diurnal rhythm disruption persisted for
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=148) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
months to even years after the initial period of adversity.
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=161) width=197.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Dysregulation of circadian HPA axis rhythms has consistently
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=277 y=174) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
been characterized as a neuroendocrinological phenotype of depres-
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=186) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sion in animal and human studies (Adam et al., 2010; Arborelius et al.
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
1999; Doane et al., 2013; Heim & Binder, 2012; Jarcho, Slavich,
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Tylova-Stein, Wolkowitz, & Burke, 2013; Mangold, Marino, &
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=225) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
Javors, 2011; Plotsky, Owens, & Nemeroff, 1998; Vrshek-Schallhorn
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=239) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
et al., 2013). Prior research has reported that depressed patients and
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=252) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
individuals at high risk of depression tend to exhibit altered evening
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=264) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol concentrations and cortisol awakening responses (CAR)
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=278) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
(Adam et al., 2017; Engert et al., 2011; Pruessner, Hellhammer,
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=291) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Pruessner, & Lupien, 2003). For instance, elevated levels of evening
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=303) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol have been reported to be characteristic of major depressive
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
disorder (MDD) in both adolescents (Angold & Worthman, 1993; Dahl
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
et al., 1991; Goodyer et al., 1996) and adults (Gold, Goodwin, &
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=342) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Chrousos, 1988; Plotsky et al., 1998; Young et al., 1994). Similarly,
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=355) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
higher CAR levels have been associated longitudinally with a height-
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ened risk of developing MDD (Adam et al., 2010). Cross-sectional
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
studies have previously reported positive associations between CAR
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=394) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
levels and depressive symptoms (Dahl et al., 1991; Pruessner
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
et al., 2003). Although not all depressed patients display HPA axis dys-
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
regulation (Heim, Newport, Mletzko, Miller, & Nemeroff, 2008), these
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=433) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
alterations in diurnal cortisol rhythms are thought to influence depres-
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sion, possibly as a result of inflammation caused by glucocorticoid
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
receptor insensitivity (Miller, Maletic, & Raison, 2009) and impairment
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=472) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of glucocorticoid-mediated negative feedback processes of the HPA
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=485) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
axis (Pariante & Lightman, 2008). Thus, prior research has emphasized
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
that alterations in HPA axis function are not only affected by the
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=511) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
embodiment of early life and recent social experiences but may also
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=524) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
predict individual risk for depression later in life.
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=538) width=166.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The role of the HPA axis in shaping the depressive effects of early
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=277 y=550) width=228.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
life stress, however, remains unclear as results from past studies have
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=563) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
reported mixed findings (Engert et al., 2011; Gerritsen et al., 2010;
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Gonzalez et al., 2009; Mangold et al., 2011; Zilioli et al., 2016).
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Although growing evidence has implicated early life stress-induced
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=602) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
alterations in HPA axis function as a possible mechanism for adult
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
depression, the potential psychobiological mechanisms that underlie
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the long-term depressive effects of early life stress are not well-
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=641) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
known. Additionally, these studies have mostly been conducted
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=654) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
among Western and industrialized populations, where cultural, eco-
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
nomic, and psychosocial environments vary in comparison to low- and
page=2 block=-122531643 (x=262 y=680) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
middle-income contexts, where the burden of mental illness is the

page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=135) width=164.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Our aim in the current study is to explore whether the role of
page=3 block=15938268 (x=17 y=148) width=227.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
early life stress as a predictor of adult depression is accounted for by
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=161) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
alterations in adult HPA axis function in a non-clinical, naturalistic set-
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=174) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ting. Data come from the Cebu Longitudinal Health and Nutrition Sur-
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=187) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
vey (CLHNS), a longitudinal population-based birth cohort study
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
located in metropolitan Cebu, Philippines (Adair et al. 2011). Recent
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
prevalence estimates of depression in the Philippines calculate that
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=226) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
~3.3 million adults, or ~3–4% of the total population, are affected by
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=238) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
depression (WHO, 2017), and some cross-sectional studies report

higher rates in higher risk groups such as those living in low income
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=265) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
communities (22.5%) (Flores, Tuazon, Hernandez, &
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=278) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
Evangelista, 2017). Between 2007 and 2017, depressive disorders
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=291) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
accounted for the eighth-leading cause of disability in the Philippines
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=304) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(IHME, 2017). Previous research studies from the CLHNS sample have
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
reported dramatically higher rates of depression among individuals
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
who witnessed domestic violence growing up (Hindin &
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=343) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=11
Gultiano, 2006) and that lifecourse socioeconomic status predicts

altered adult diurnal cortisol rhythms (DeSantis et al., 2015). We build
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
on this work to test the hypothesis that a composite measure of early
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
life stressors will predict depressive symptoms during early adulthood.
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Additionally, we further hypothesize that altered HPA axis function,
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as reflected by elevated salivary bedtime cortisol and cortisol awaken-
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ing response levels, will account for the depressive effects of early life
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
stress.
page=3 block=15938268 (x=1 y=447) width=22.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
2 | METHODS
page=3 block=5770072 (x=1 y=484) width=88.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=14
2.1 | Study population and data collection
page=3 block=13241332 (x=1 y=510) width=202.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Data come from the Cebu Longitudinal Health and Nutrition Survey
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(CLHNS), an ongoing, population-based birth cohort study of mothers
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and their infants born in 1983–1984 in Cebu, Philippines (Adair
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=563) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
et al., 2010). A single stage, random clustering sampling procedure
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
selected 17 urban and 16 rural communities (barangays) among the
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
243 barangays in the metropolitan Cebu area, resulting in the recruit-
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ment of 3,327 pregnant women. Initial and follow-up surveys were
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
conducted through question-based, in-home interviews. Data for this
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
analysis come from survey rounds conducted in 1991–1992,
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=641) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
1994–1995, 1998, 2002, and 2005 (Adair et al., 2010). All interviews
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=654) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
were completed in Cebuano. Complete socio-demographic, psychoso-
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
cial, mental health, and cortisol data were available for 1,244 partici-
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=681) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
pants. Participants with highly atypical sleep patterns were excluded
page=3 block=16011328 (x=1 y=694) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
those of shift workers, are known to exhibit altered diurnal cortisol
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=31) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
rhythms (Kudielka, Buchtal, Uhde, & Wust, 2007; Niu et al., 2011).
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=44) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Comparisons of our analytic sample with the larger cohort sample

from the 2005 collection not included in this analysis show nonsignifi-

cant differences in household income at birth and 2005, the fre-

quency of sibling death, feeling distant from one's father, and

depressive symptom severity (all p > .3). Household income at birth
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=109) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and 2005 and depressive symptoms were lower in the analytical sam-

ple relative to the excluded sample, while the frequencies of sibling

death and feelingdistant from one's father were greater in the analyti-
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=148) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
cal sample. Conversely, education levels at 2005 (p = .0067) and

assets at 2005 (p = .0398) were higher in the analytical sample, while
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=174) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
maternal absence (p = .00001), paternal absence (p = .00001), and
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=187) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
feeling close to one's mother (p = .0141) occurred more frequently in
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=200) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the excluded sample. The analytical sample tended to witness parental
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
violence at a greater proportion (p = .075). Thus, the analytic sample is
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=226) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
slightly more advantaged than the larger CLHNS cohort. This research
page=3 block=-106618888 (x=263 y=239) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
was conducted under conditions of written informed consent with

University of North Carolina, Chapel Hill, and Northwestern

2.2|Measuresofearlylifestressanddepressive
page=3 block=-296216488 (x=263 y=328) width=232.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
symptoms
page=3 block=-296216488 (x=263 y=341) width=45.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
Six measures of early life family-related stress were operationalized as
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=369) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
dichotomous categorical variables and summed to create a composite
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
score. Early life stress variables included measures of maternal
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
absence, paternal instability, sibling death, witnessing domestic vio-
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=408) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lence during childhood (Gettler et al 2015; Hindin & Gultiano, 2006),
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=421) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and reverse scored measures of feeling close to one's mother during
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
childhood, and closeness to one's father during childhood
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=10
(Hindin, 2005). Participants were classified as having experienced
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
maternal absence if they lived in a separate household than their
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
mother at 8.5 years old in 1991 or 11.5 years old in 1994. Individuals
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=486) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
who were classified as having experienced “paternal instability” were
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=498) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
those whose father was deceased or absent, whose mother was
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=512) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
unmarried during their first year of life or beyond, or whose mother
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=525) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
remarried during their childhood-juvenile period from birth up to age
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
11.5 (±0.4) years, which was in 1994. Sibling death was assessed
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
based upon maternal reports of her children's births and deaths from
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
1983 until 1994 when participants were 11.5 years old, also in 1994.
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=577) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Finally, participants during the 2002 data collection wave (17–
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=589) width=242.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
19 years of age) were asked “Do you remember if either of your
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=602) width=242.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
parents/caretakers ever hit, slapped, kicked, or used other means like
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
pushing or shoving to try to hurt the other physically when you were
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
growing up?” as a measure of witnessing domestic violence during
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=641) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
childhood.
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=654) width=36.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=15
Additionally, we evaluated the impact of negative parental rela-
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=278 y=668) width=227.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tionship as reflected in a measure of parental support. Prior research
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=681) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
has reported poorer mental health outcomes during adulthood when
page=3 block=-106505671 (x=263 y=694) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=4 block=1966084 (x=1 y=1) width=30.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
451
page=4 block=387710980 (x=493 y=1) width=12.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=13
Kessler, & Shaver, 1997; Nickerson, Bryant, Aderka, Hinton, &
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=31) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Hofmann, 2013). Negative parenting measures were similarly limited
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=44) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and consisted of two variables that measured parental support and
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=57) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
were reverse scored, specifically questions that asked whether the
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=70) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
participant felt close to one's mother and father during childhood dur-
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=83) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ing the 2002 data collection when children were 17–19 years old
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=95) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(Hock et al., 2016). Domestic violence questions were administered in
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=109) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
2002 as well. Whether an individual witnessed domestic violence and
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=122) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
felt close to one's parents were retrospective reports of the individ-
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=135) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ual's experience growing up, while measures of maternal absence,
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=148) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
paternal absence, and sibling death were assessed at the time of data
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=161) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
collection. After the six categorical stress variables were summed, the
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=174) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
composite variable was recoded to four groups: 0, 1, 2, and 3+
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=187) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
stressors. Each level of this variable was dummy coded in order to
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
examine dose–response or threshold effects. These six variables were
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=212) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
among the small handful of data points on early life stress that were
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=226) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
collected during the study, which was initially designed to study the
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=239) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
long-term health impacts of infant feeding.
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=252) width=147.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Depressive symptoms were measured using an adapted version
page=4 block=15937865 (x=16 y=265) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of the Center for Epidemiologic Studies Depression Scale (CES-D),
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=278) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
which is a commonly utilized mental health battery used to screen for
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=291) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
depression and assess depressive symptomatology (Radloff, 1977).
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=304) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The CES-D was administered during the 2005 data collection wave
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
for the CLHNS when participants were 21–22 years old. This survey
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=329) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
queries both negative (e.g., difficulty eating and sleeping, feeling
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=343) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
lonely, suicidal thoughts, etc.) and positive experiences (e.g., feeling
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=355) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
happy, hopeful about the future, enjoyed daily activities, etc.). Ratings
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
on a three-point scale ranged from “none of the time” (0), “occasion-
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=381) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ally” (1), to “most or all of the time” (2). Scores were summed across all
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=394) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
questions, following reverse scoring of positive items, to generate an
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
index of depressive symptoms. Cronbach's ? was .7338, suggesting an
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=420) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
acceptable degree of scale reliability.
page=4 block=15937865 (x=1 y=434) width=127.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
2.3|Cortisolcollection,preparation,and
page=4 block=12919541 (x=1 y=471) width=197.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
measurement
page=4 block=12919541 (x=1 y=484) width=60.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=19
During the 2005 survey, each participant was provided with instruc-
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=512) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tions and three polypropylene tubes for saliva collection. Three sam-
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=525) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ples representing cortisol levels at key points in time in the diurnal
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol cycle were collected. The first was collected at bedtime, the
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
next immediately upon waking, and the final sample 30 min after wak-
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ing. This protocol allowed us to capture both morning and evening
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol levels in a limited timeframe; we believe any error
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
(or additional state variation) introduced by sampling evening levels
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
on 1 day and morning levels on the next day is compensated for by
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the large sample size (~1,250 participants) associated with this study
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(Adam & Kumari, 2009). Each tube was pre-labeled to avoid confusion
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=642) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and contamination, and participants were further instructed to keep
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=655) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the other two empty tubes in a Ziploc bag beside their bed to more
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
easily facilitate the collection of the second saliva sample immediately
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=681) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
upon waking. Participants were also given timers to set to 30 min, at
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=694) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
which time they provided the third sample, and were instructed not to
page=4 block=16027136 (x=1 y=707) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
brush their teeth or smoke 30 min before providing any samples. Par-
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=31) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ticipants were also provided diaries in which they were instructed to
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=44) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
record the date and exact time the samples were provided (Adam &
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=57) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Kumari, 2009).
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=70) width=51.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The cortisol awakening response was estimated by subtracting
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=277 y=83) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the waking cortisol values from the levels 30 min later, although we
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=96) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
recognize that this calculation of CAR no longer meets the guidelines
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=109) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
for optimal measurement (Stalder et al., 2016). All cortisol values were
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=122) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
natural log transformed to correct strong positive skews, and mea-
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=135) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sures in this analysis were standardized such that effect sizes could be
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=148) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
easily compared across different cortisol measures. Diurnal slopes
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=161) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
were calculated by subtracting waking cortisol levels from evening
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=174) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
levels and dividing this value by the length of the waking day in hours
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=186) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
using data from both days of saliva sampling (DeSantis et al., 2015).
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
An hourly rate of cortisol decline from morning levels to evening
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
levels was therefore calculated, with steeper slopes reflected by a
page=4 block=-122545748 (x=262 y=226) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
larger, more negative rate of decline, and flatter slopes associated

Saliva tubes were collected later the second day by an inter-
page=6 block=239076596 (x=16 y=317) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
viewer, who placed the tubes on ice packs in a cooler while in transit
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to freezer storage at ?35C. Samples were shipped on dry ice to
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=339) width=243.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Northwestern University, where they were stored at ?80C. They
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=352) width=243.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
were later thawed, centrifuged, supernatant separated, and aliquoted
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
into smaller tubes for analysis of individual analytes. Cortisol was
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
assayed in duplicate by a laboratory in Trier, Germany, using a time-
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
resolved immunoassay with fluorometric detection (DELFIA). The
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
intra- and inter-assay coefficients of variation (CVs) were between 4.0
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and 6.7%, and 7.1 to 9.0%, respectively. Samples with CVs over 12%
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
were rerun. Our analyses examined cortisol in log-transformed micro-
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
gram per deciliter (?g/dl) units.
page=6 block=15969468 (x=1 y=459) width=105.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
2.4 | Covariates
page=6 block=5704614 (x=1 y=497) width=87.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=14
Covariates included sex, smoking status, time of evening saliva col-
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=525) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lection (Kudielka & Kirschbaum, 2003), and two measures of socio-
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
economic status. Food consumption and exercise were also tested
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as covariates but were not included in the final models since they
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
were not significantly related to early life stress, depressive symp-
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
toms, or individual cortisol indices. Socioeconomic status (SES) was
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
assessed using a multidimensional summary measure, which was
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
comprised of the following variables: household income (weekly
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
income in pesos standardized to year), a household assets (sum of
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=629) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the following items: home ownership, electricity in the home, type of
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=642) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
housing material, and ownership of items such as air conditioner,
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=655) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
television, refrigerator, or car), and educational attainment at both
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
years (years of formal schooling). Each variable was standardized
page=6 block=16016073 (x=1 y=681) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(mean = 0, SD = 1), and values were averaged to create the summary

In order to capture the overall SES environment during the
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=277 y=317) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
timespan in which the experiences of early life stress occurred,
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
between infancy and middle childhood, an “early life SES” variable
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=342) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
was constructed by summing SES scores from the 1983, 1986, 1991,
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=356) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and 1994 surveys. Pairwise correlations indicate a high level of agree-
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ment between SES in 1983, 1986, 1991, and 1994 as the correlation
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
coefficient ranged between R = 0.81 and R = 0.89, further justifying
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the construction of a summary early SES variable. For the young
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
adulthood SES variable, participants' own education (years of formal
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
schooling) was used in place of parental education. Cronbach's ? for
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=433) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
each survey was as follows: .72 (1983), .74 (1986), .78 (1991), and
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
.56 (2005).
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=460) width=37.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
To control for the effects of recent stress, self-reported psy-
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=277 y=473) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
chosocial stress experienced within the last month was quantified
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=485) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
using a modified version of the 10-item perceived stress-scale
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(PSS) (Cohen, Kamarck, & Mermelstein, 1994). To ensure cultural
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=512) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
appropriateness, all stress related questions were pilot tested and
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=524) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
refined in focus groups. Participants were asked to rate on a five-
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
point scale (0 = never, 4 = very often) how often they experienced
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
a particular form of stress. The PSS was administered in Cebuano
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
after being translated from English and back-translated to confirm
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
accuracy. As a result of preliminary research with the PSS in Cebu,
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
prior to implementation in the 2005 survey, two questions (item
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
9, “how often have you been angered”; item 10, “how often have
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=615) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
you felt difficulties were piling up”) were replaced with two new
page=6 block=-122433883 (x=262 y=628) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
questions: “In the last 4 weeks, how often have you dealt success-

2.5 | Data analyses
page=7 block=6686952 (x=1 y=380) width=102.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
All analyses were conducted using version 13.1 of Stata (Stata Corpo-
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ration, College Station, TX). Depressive symptom scores, PSS, waking
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol, cortisol awakening response, bedtime cortisol, diurnal cortisol
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
slope, household income, household assets, educational attainment,
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and time of evening saliva collection were all analyzed as continuous
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
variables. The data analysis plan consisted of four stages. First,
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
bivariate associations between early life stress, cortisol measures,
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=486) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
depressive symptom scores, and covariates were calculated
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
(Tables 2a and 2b). Second, we considered sex as well as past and
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=512) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
recent household SES as predictors. Our final model examined
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=525) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
whether or not associations between early life stress and depressive
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
symptoms are dependent on our HPA axis measures by including mea-
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sures of PM cortisol and the cortisol awakening response, then diurnal
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
slope and the cortisol awakening response. Diurnal slope and bedtime
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol were not included in the same model because both variables
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
strongly covaried with one another (Table 2b). All cortisol models were
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
adjusted for time of saliva collection and smoking. ? < .05 was used as
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=615) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the criterion for statistical significance.
page=7 block=16017048 (x=1 y=629) width=131.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

participants did not feel close with their mother during adolescence
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=382) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
while 26% of individuals did not feel close with their father during
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adolescence. A smaller proportion of respondents had a sibling die

page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
uals experienced at least one stressful event and 92 adults
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=447) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
experienced three or more stressful events. The average adapted
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
depressive symptom score in the analytic sample was 9.3/29. Bivari-
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ate associations between major study variables are presented in
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=486) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Table 2a. Table 2b shows zero-order correlations between dis-
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=499) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
aggregated early life stress measures and cortisol indices.

Our first model (Model 1) assessed the relationship between our

page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
21–22. Results from this partial regression model also show a dose–
page=7 block=-106506360 (x=263 y=576) width=242.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
response relationship between the number of early life stressors and

Mazure, 2001; Nolen-Hoeksema, 2001; Weissman et al., 1993).

awakening response did not reach significance. Including the cortisol

variables modestly altered the relationship between early life stress

and depressive symptoms as indicated by the change in the coeffi-

cient of the early life stress composite variable from Model 2 (0.4006)

to Model 4 (.353), the fully adjusted model, and the dummy coded

Because the measure of diurnal cortisol slope is calculated by

in time (Adam & Kumari, 2009; DeSantis et al., 2015), we dis-

ning levels to examine which component of the diurnal cortisol

rhythm impacted depression risk (Models 5 and 6). The new fully-

age, evening cortisol levels significantly predict adult depressive

symptoms (p = .004) (Figure 1). Greater evening cortisol concentra-

tions correspond with higher depressive symptom scores (Table 3a).

The effect of waking cortisol on adult depressive symptom is not sig-

nificant. Early life stress continues to significantly predict adult

depressive symptoms in a dose–response fashion. Similar to the

effects of diurnal slope and CAR variables in Models 3 and 4, the

modest changes in the coefficient of the early life stress composite

variable from Model 2 (0.4006) to Model 6 (0.39), the complete

model, and the dummy coded stress groups.
page=8 block=-122542121 (x=262 y=83) width=152.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
We then disaggregated the early life stress composite measure to

page=8 block=-122542121 (x=262 y=122) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(Table 3b). One categorical predictor variable was removed to serve

as the reference group. Witnessing parental violence significantly

vidual's depressive symptom score by an average of 0.75 points in

fully adjusted models (p < .0001) (Figure 2). No other early life stress
page=8 block=-122542121 (x=262 y=186) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
measures significantly predicted depression risk. Being male and
page=8 block=-122542121 (x=262 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
greater socioeconomic status at 2005, but not during early life, cor-
page=8 block=-122542121 (x=262 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
responded with decreased depressive symptom severity. Similar to

the previous model, higher levels of evening cortisol significantly asso-
page=8 block=-122542121 (x=262 y=239) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ciated with higher depressive symptom scores (p = .005).
page=8 block=-122542121 (x=262 y=252) width=196.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Finally, we conducted a separate analysis to examine whether
page=8 block=-122542121 (x=277 y=264) width=228.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
early life stress affected young adult cortisol rhythms. Our results

4 | DISCUSSION
page=8 block=1717045558 (x=262 y=393) width=100.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=13
Our findings suggest that early life stress, witnessing domestic vio-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lence during childhood, and alterations in adult HPA axis function pre-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
dict the severity of depressive symptoms during adulthood in this
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
sample of Cebuano young adults. However, contrary to previous find-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ings in the literature, these relationships appeared to be independent
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of one another, as indicated by the modest changes in the relationship
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=486) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
between early life stress and adult depression risk after controlling for
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
HPA axis measures (Tables 3a and 3b). Thus, we report that diurnal
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=512) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol activity does not affect adult depressive symptoms as a result
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=525) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of early life stress during child development. Our early life stress com-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
posite measure also did not predict diurnal cortisol patterns during
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
youngadulthood in this sample, but witnessing domestic violence dur-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ing childhood predicted lower bedtime cortisol levels.
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=577) width=184.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Our findings linking early life stress and diurnal cortisol rhythms
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=277 y=590) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
with depression are in general agreement with past work investigating
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
similar questions. We confirm the overall positive association previ-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ously reported between early life stress and adult depressive symp-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
toms (Heim & Binder, 2012; Taylor et al., 2011) as seen by the direct
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=642) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
relationship between our composite measure of early life stress and
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=654) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adult depressive symptoms (Figure 3) as well as the long-term adult
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
depressive impacts of witnessing domestic violence during childhood.
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=681) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
When examining the relationships between each individual, dis-
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=694) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
aggregated stress measure and young adult depression risk, with
page=8 block=-122430230 (x=262 y=707) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
