page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=161) width=93.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Our results also report that flatter adult diurnal cortisol slopes,
page=10 block=15945925 (x=16 y=174) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
but not concentrations of waking cortisol or the cortisol awakening
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=187) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
response, are directly associated with adult depressive symptoms.
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
This finding is consistent with an increasing body of literature that
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
strongly suggeststhat flatter diurnal cortisol rhythms may be a distinct
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=226) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
characteristic of certain forms of depression and other physical and
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=239) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
mental diseases (Adam, 2012; Balardin, Vedana, Luz, &
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=252) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=12
Bromberg, 2011; Bhattacharyya, Molloy, & Steptoe, 2008; Jarcho
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=265) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
et al., 2013; Knight, Avery, Janssen, & Powell, 2010; Weinrib
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=278) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
et al., 2010). Past studies have found that flatter diurnal cortisol
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=291) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
rhythms occur due to either lower morning cortisol, higher evening
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=304) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol, or both, all of which are indications of HPA axis dysregulation
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(DeSantis et al., 2015; Doane et al., 2013; Lopez-Duran et al. 2009;
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Weinrib et al., 2010), a strongly implicated mechanism of adult
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=343) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
depression (Adam et al., 2010; Arborelius et al. 1999; Doane
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=355) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
et al., 2013; Heim & Binder, 2012; Jarcho et al., 2013; Mangold
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
et al., 2011; Plotsky et al., 1998; Vrshek-Schallhorn et al., 2013). Dis-
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
aggregating the slope measure showed that higher evening cortisol
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
levels predominantly accounted for the effects of cortisol diurnal
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
rhythms on adult depression symptomatology (Figure 1). The positive
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
association between evening cortisol concentrations and adult
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
depressive symptoms is also consistent with previous studies (Gold
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
et al., 1988; Plotsky et al., 1998; Young et al., 1994). While we did not
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
find any significant links between the cortisol awakening response
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and depressive symptoms, prior research has reported altered cortisol
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=485) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
awakening responses among depressed adults. These findings tend to
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
report elevated cortisol awakening responses during depression
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=512) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
(Bhagwagar, Hafizi, & Cowen, 2003; Pruessner et al., 2003), though
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=524) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

Ellenbogen et al. 2006; van der Vegt et al., 2009). Variations in the neu-
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
roendocrine profiles of depressed patients across studies may relate to

the severity of depression considered in each study, which ranges from
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
heightened depressed mood within the normal range of symptomatol-
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ogy tomajor depressive disorder (Chida & Steptoe, 2009).
page=10 block=15945925 (x=1 y=603) width=196.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
We also find that individuals who witnessed domestic violence

during adolescence tended to have lower bedtime cortisol levels at

response. The association between witnessing domestic violence and

lower bedtime cortisol levels was an unexpected finding as past stud-

greater evening levels. For example, Engert et al. (2011) and Nicol-

care and experienced a loss of a parent either by death or separation

exhibited higher bedtime cortisol levels during adulthood. Addition-

study, the association between decreased evening cortisol levels dur-

cence may be a function of the timing since the onset of stress. In the

page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=252) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
qualities of the stressor that may explain our discrepant finding.
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=265) width=220.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Considering the inverse relationship between witnessing domes-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=17 y=278) width=227.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tic violence and adult evening cortisol levels, the finding that bedtime
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=291) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
cortisol levels do not influence the relationship between early life
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=304) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
stress and depressive symptoms is expected statistically but concep-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tually perplexing. The strengthened relationship between early life
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
stress and adult depressive symptoms after including the bedtime cor-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=343) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tisol variable suggests that early life stress predicts depressive symp-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=355) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
toms despite the fact that individuals in our sample tend to exhibit
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cortisol levels of non-depressed individuals. We intended on con-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ducting a mediation analysis to evaluate the links between early life
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stress, cortisol levels, and depressive risk, but our results did not meet
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the criteria for mediation analysis as all three variables were not sig-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
nificantly correlated to one another. While cross-sectional measure of
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
HPA axis function may not be related to the depressive effects of
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
early life stress during young adulthood, HPA axis function at earlier
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stages of development may still influence one's risk of later life
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
depression (Adam et al., 2010; Bockting et al., 2012; Goodyer, Bacon,
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=485) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Ban, Croudace, & Herbert, 2009; Vrshek-Schallhorn et al., 2013).
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
These findings also suggest that additional biological mechanisms may
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=512) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
facilitate the durable effects of early life experiences on later life
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=524) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
health. Other biological pathways of HPA axis function that have pre-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
viously been implicated in the early life stress-adult depression path-
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
way include upstream mechanisms like epigenetic variation (e.g., DNA
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=563) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
methylation, histone modification) (Heim & Binder, 2012; Khulan
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
et al., 2014; Smith, Parets, & Kim, 2014) and downstream pathways
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
such as inflammatory (immune activation, gut-brain axis) (Pace
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
et al., 2006) and neurobiological mechanisms (e.g., neurotransmission,
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
neuroanatomic changes) (Gatt et al., 2010; Grant, Cannistraci, Hollon,
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Gore, & Shelton, 2011). For example, early life stress has been
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=642) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
hypothesized to influence differential methylation signatures among
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=654) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
HPA axis-related genes, such as the glucocorticoid receptor gene,
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
which in may alter the binding of cortisol and its subsequent impact
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=681) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
on adult depression (Heim & Binder, 2012; Jawahar, Murgatroyd,
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=693) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Harrison, & Baune, 2015).
page=11 block=15946328 (x=1 y=707) width=92.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Future studies can further elucidate the role of HPA axis function
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=278 y=31) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in contributing to the long-term effects of early life stress on adult
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=44) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
depressive symptomatology by waiting to evaluate adult depressive
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=56) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
risk a few months or years after cortisol measurements are collected.
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=70) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
This longitudinal design can help clarify whether the durability of
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=83) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
altered adult cortisol activity due to early life stress is what influences
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=95) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
later life depressive symptoms and eliminates the possibility of
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=109) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
reverse causality between cross-sectional measures of depressive
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=122) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
symptoms and cortisol levels, as seen in our study. Reliable and cultur-
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=134) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ally validated measures of early life stress and depression will also
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=148) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
strengthen the internal validity of future studies, and multiple days of
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=161) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
cortisol collection with daily assessments of mood, behaviors, and
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=174) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
experience will better characterize typical diurnal cortisol rhythms.
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=186) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Finally, additional stress-sensitive mechanism that are both upstream
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and downstream of HPA axis function can be assessed to identify
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
other biological pathways involved in the long-term depressive effects
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=225) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of early life stress.
page=11 block=-106620500 (x=263 y=239) width=62.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
4.1 | Limitations
page=11 block=1534002808 (x=263 y=276) width=89.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=14
The first limitation of this study is our crude measure of early life
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=304) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stress. Our variables were limited to family-level experiences and con-
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ditions that were reduced to only six dummy variables. There are
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
likely a wide variety of stressors across multiple domains and levels of
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=343) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
life that may have similar long-term effects on mental health and, in
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=356) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
general, affect the body. Different dimensions of psychosocial stress,
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=369) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
such as a more diverse set of experiences and conditions (various
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
forms of social oppression, disease and illness experience, teratogens,
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=395) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
etc.), chronicity, severity, and sociocultural meaning, could have dis-
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=408) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tinct and varied sequelae on HPA axis function (e.g., diurnal cortisol
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
rhythm, stress reactivity) and later life health.
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=434) width=155.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The cultural relevance and psychometric validity of the depres-
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=278 y=447) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sion screener used in this study (i.e., CES-D) may be another source of
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=460) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
bias. While the Cronbach's alpha meets the threshold for scale valid-
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=473) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ity, the comprehensibility and relevance of the sub-constructs
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=485) width=242.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
included in the CES-D may be compromised because of their lack of
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=499) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cultural relevance and salience. The same concern applies to other
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=512) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
survey tools used in this study, such as the Perceived Stress Scale.
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=525) width=228.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Third, our process of sampling and measuring the cortisol awak-
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=278 y=538) width=227.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ening response did not meet expert guidelines as outlined Stalder
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
et al. (2016). Only two post-awakening samples were obtained to
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
assess the CAR (at waking and 30 min later), rather than the rec-
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=577) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ommended minimum of three. In addition, cortisol data were collected
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=590) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
across 2 days—the first at bedtime and the next two immediately
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=602) width=242.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
upon waking and 30 min later. Because momentary- and day-level
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=616) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
changes in emotions and social experiences shape cortisol levels
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
(Adam, 2006), it is preferred to collect samples for diurnal cortisol
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=642) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
across multiple days (Adam & Kumari, 2009). Additionally, in order to
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=654) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
maximize sample efficiency in this large, longitudinal sample for which
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=668) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
home-based sample refrigeration was not available, the diurnal
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=681) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
rhythm was first assessed at bedtime and followed into the next day.
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=693) width=242.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Low compliance to saliva collection instructions at specific timepoints
page=11 block=-106503344 (x=263 y=707) width=242.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

459
page=12 block=387710980 (x=493 y=1) width=12.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=13
may also bias cortisol data. Further analyses show, however, that
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=31) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
there were no significant or considerable differences between the

time of collection between the wake +30 min sample and the wake
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=57) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sample among individuals with low and high levels of depressive
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=70) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
symptoms, steeper and flatter slopes, and lower and higher CARs.
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=83) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Finally, we did not assess mood states, daytime experiences, and
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=96) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
stress levels at the times of saliva sampling nor utilize electronically
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=109) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
monitor compliance with sampling protocols (Stalder et al., 2016). As
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=122) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
part of a large population-based study, however, errors in reliability
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=135) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
due to this minimized protocol are likely to be reduced by the large
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=148) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
sample size (Adam & Kumari, 2009). Because the ongoing study
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=161) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
already included an extensive protocol and was conducted across a
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=174) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
large metropolitan area and many surrounding rural communities, the
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=187) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
research team felt that reduced reliability was a reasonable tradeoff
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=200) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
for the large sample size that is a representative 1-year birth cohort
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=213) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
for the country's second-largest city.
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=226) width=126.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
tion Studies, University of San Carlos, Cebu, Philippines, for their role
page=12 block=15946328 (x=1 y=707) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
5 | CONCLUSION
page=12 block=6948394 (x=1 y=263) width=106.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=EGHBLN+AdvTT6071803a.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=13
In summary, early life family-related stressors predicted greater severity
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=291) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of depressive symptoms at early adulthood in a dose–response fashion
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=303) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
and witnessing domestic violence during child development predicted
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=317) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lower levels of evening cortisol in this large, naturalistic, non-Western,
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=330) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and longitudinal sample of individuals in metropolitanCebu, Philippines.
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=343) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
When considering the cumulative effects of early life family-based
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=356) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
adversity, greater exposure to these stressors during development
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=369) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
predicted worse depressive outcomes in early adulthood. While both
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=382) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
bedtime cortisol and diurnal cortisol slopes significantly predicted adult
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=395) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
depressive symptoms cross-sectionally, adult cortisol levels did not
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=408) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
explain the effect of early life stress on adult depressive symptoms.
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=421) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Furthermore, this analysis does not support the notion that the depres-
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=434) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sive impacts of early life stress are influenced by HPA axis function in
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=447) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
this particular stage of young adulthood and this sample. Given our lim-
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=460) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ited saliva collection protocols and that we measured cortisol at only
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=473) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
one stage of adulthood, more robust measures of HPA axis function
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=485) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
across multiple time points throughout development may uncover pos-
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=499) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sible sensitive periods that may be involved in the psychobiological
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=512) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
development of adult depression. Additionally, we also note that our
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=525) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
early life stress compositevariable is limited as it is not a validated mea-
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=538) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
sure of family-based stress in Cebu. Nonetheless, these findings sug-
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=551) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
gest that the long-term depressive effects of certain forms of early life
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=564) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
stress extend to this large sample in the Philippines, and that early life
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=577) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stress and HPA axis function at age 21–22 in this sample may shape
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=589) width=243.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
adult depression through independent pathways in our sample. Our
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=603) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
results provide further evidence that HPA axis activity is shaped by
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=616) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
early life conditions and is associated with depressive symptoms, and
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=629) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that early life development is an important period research and inter-
page=12 block=16032336 (x=1 y=642) width=243.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=EGHCBM+AdvTTa9c1b374 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
vention for improving globalmental health.
